# Frozen tree-Gibbs trajectory discovery

Owner: max3. One continuation round, completed ceiling 44 / attempted 45.
Authorization: TASK.md and master_guidance c3dd5f8ea6c8852a. All older
ledgers remain closed. This is numerical discovery, not a verified theorem.
No fact submission, solver, benchmark DP, installation, or extra word.

## Fixed mathematical object and direct derivation

For enclosing m in {2,6,10,14}, put lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m,
c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c. Define H_0 empty and
H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), j=0,...,m-1, keeping this SAME m throughout.
Thus n=2^m-1. Equivalently physical h_i=1+a*c^v2(i+1), 0<=i<n,
where v2(k) is the exponent of 2 dividing the positive integer k.
The equivalence follows by splitting the sequence at its central odd bridge:
left and right halves repeat H_(m-1), and the central index has v2=m-1.

Put r_tree=2/c, Zgeom=sum_(j<m)r_tree^j, w_j=r_tree^j/Zgeom. The full
space has an orthonormal coordinate for every internal binary-tree node
v of depth less than m. For each leaf s in {0,1}^m, the feature a_s has
coordinate sqrt(w_j)*(-1)^s_(j+1) at its depth-j ancestor and zero
elsewhere. Define F(x)=log(2^-m sum_s exp(<a_s,x>)).

The feature mean is zero: at each coordinate the leaves in its left and
right halves cancel. Distinct coordinate rows are orthogonal, since they
have disjoint support or the descendant's signs cancel inside an ancestor's
constant-sign support. Each row has positive norm. Therefore the feature
matrix has full row rank. Jensen gives F>=0, with equality precisely when
all leaf logits coincide; their mean is zero, and full row rank then forces
x=0. Thus the full minimizer is uniquely 0 and F_star=0.

Differentiation of the finite sum gives gradient E_p a and Hessian
Cov_p(a). For a real random variable in [u,v], its variance is at most
(v-u)^2/4: its second moment about the midpoint is at most that number,
and variance is the minimum second moment about a constant. Applying this
to every unit projection bounds the Hessian by one quarter of the squared
feature diameter. Leaves first diverging at depth j have squared distance
4w_j+2 sum_(k>j)w_k. The corresponding quarter distances decrease with j,
because w_j-w_(j+1)/2>0. Their maximum is (1+w_0)/2, denoted Ltree.
This is a globally valid gradient Lipschitz constant. It is also sharp:
the full row rank maps onto all zero-mean leaf logits, allowing probability
to approach equal mass on any selected pair of root-diverging leaves.
The covariance in their difference direction approaches Ltree.

Initialize only the left-spine coordinates x_(0^j)=log(2)/sqrt(w_j);
all other coordinates are zero. The TRUE initial squared radius is
R2=(log 2)^2 sum_j 1/w_j=(log 2)^2 r_tree^(1-m) Zgeom^2.
The second equality is the finite geometric-sum reversal identity.

A first-right-turn group at depth k<m contains 2^(m-k-1) leaves;
the final all-left group k=m contains one leaf. Its uniform reference
mass is q_k=2^(-k-1) for k<m and q_m=2^-m. If y_j=sqrt(w_j)x_(0^j),
their logits are ell_k=sum_(j<k)y_j-y_k for k<m and ell_m=sum_j y_j.
All nodes off the spine lie inside one group. When their coordinates
vanish, its leaves have equal logits and equal probabilities; hence its
two descendant halves give zero gradient on that coordinate. Induction
proves these coordinates remain zero at EVERY physical query.

Consequently P_k=q_k exp(ell_k)/sum_l q_l exp(ell_l), and the actual
spine gradient has coordinate sqrt(w_j)d_j, where
d_j=sum_(k>j)P_k-P_j. The complete actual step is
y'_j=y_j-(h_i*w_j/Ltree)d_j. No child restart or transition labels occur.
Initially q_k exp(ell_k)=1/4 for k<m and 1 for k=m; thus
P_k=1/(m+4), P_m=4/(m+4), F(x0)=log((m+4)/4).
Also sum_k q_k ell_k=0, so F=sum_k q_k log(q_k/P_k).

## Stable evaluation of this identical finite function

Let G_m=0 be the log partition of the final empty subtree. Splitting the
depth-j subtree into its right zero-field group and left spine gives
G_j=log((exp(-y_j)+exp(y_j+G_(j+1)))/2)
   =G_(j+1)/2+logcosh(t_j), t_j=y_j+G_(j+1)/2.
Thus F=G_0, with every term nonnegative. Evaluate logcosh(t) as
log1p(2*sinh(t/2)^2) for |t|<=20, and as
|t|+log1p(exp(-2|t|))-log(2) otherwise. This avoids the cancellation
of subtracting log(2) or a shifted normalizer near the minimizer.

The conditional left probability at node j is sigmoid(2t_j), the right
probability sigmoid(-2t_j). Starting at reach mass U_0=1, set
P_j=U_j sigmoid(-2t_j), U_(j+1)=U_j sigmoid(2t_j), and P_m=U_m.
Both sigmoid signs use exp(-2|t_j|), never a positive large exponent.
The exact derivative difference is d_j=U_j*tanh(t_j), evaluated in that
form to avoid cancellation between almost equal probabilities.
This is an algebraic evaluation of the SAME P, not a new update rule.

The binary64 jobs also evaluate the grouped shifted-log-sum-exp objective
as a diagnostic, retaining its difference from G_0. They retain every y,
P, and actual objective at every index 0,...,n. Raw JSONL is gzip-compressed
at level 1; summary checkpoints are explicitly sampled and are not a
replacement for the complete raw trajectory. Conditional underflow,
zero probabilities, squared-small-field underflow, nonfinite output,
normalization error, and derivative-difference residuals are retained.

## Jobs and acceptance criteria frozen before any evaluation

1. m=2, binary64, full four-leaf/three-coordinate GD versus independent
   reduced GD, at every query including final index 3. Compare actual F,
   all full gradient coordinates, leaf/group probabilities, full state,
   initial radius, initial probabilities, minimum evaluation, diameter
   smoothness, and the recursively built word. Absolute tolerance 2e-12
   for trajectory quantities; radius relative tolerance 2e-12. Any failure
   blocks the larger jobs. A correction, if needed, consumes job 5 and
   must preserve the mathematical object.
2. m=6, binary64, exactly 63 physical steps, once after calibration passes.
3. m=10, binary64, exactly 1023 physical steps, once after calibration passes.
4. m=14, binary64, exactly 16383 physical steps, once after calibration passes.
5. If calibration needs no correction: independent Decimal precision-60
   check at m=10, all 1023 physical steps, using GROUPED log-sum-exp and
   direct group-tail derivatives rather than the nested binary64 evaluator.
   Parameters and schedule are rebuilt in Decimal. Compare against all
   saved binary64 queries: state/probability absolute error tolerance 1e-9,
   final objective relative error tolerance 1e-8 when nonzero. This is an
   independent numerical check, not a rigorous error enclosure.

The source files evaluate.py and run_job.py are frozen by SHA256 in
manifest.json before reservation. Every charged request records its exact
source hashes, parameters, precision, tolerances, and wall guards.
run_job.py reserves with a file lock and atomic ledger replacement BEFORE
launching evaluate.py. Each job consumes its entire 4-second cap, including
imports, model construction, evaluation, compression, and summary writing;
no refund. The child has a 3.4-second interrupt and the parent a 3.6-second
kill guard, leaving time for final accounting within the 4-second charge.
Every process exit, exception, and timeout is finally accounted. Any
unknown-time external timeout also consumes the full reservation.
There is no parallel computation. A failed discovery job is retained and
never automatically retried. Maximum 5 jobs and 20 charged seconds.
The prior guard is 850 seconds (not measured); guard plus allocation is
870, below the standing 900-second backstop. All unused credits expire
at batch closure or launch end. Older ledgers are not touched.

## References and interpretation scope

The new mathematical derivations above are UNVERIFIED source justification
within a numerical pilot, not accepted predecessors. Shared38da1067bcd1a0c8
and7ec66d4b933755c3 supplied the candidate, not mathematical premises.

The reported instance score is V=2F(x_n)/(Ltree*R2), in the project's W=2R
normalization. The exact target is B_n*V>=1, not positivity of 2^m F.
No new benchmark DP is permitted. Accepted04219554e41d9750, proof (7.1)
and (9.3), gives 4lambda^(m-1)<=B_(2^m-1)<=4lambda^m for m>=1.
The lower bound starts from B_1=4 and iterates its stated odd-index
inequality. The script evaluates these CLOSED reference bounds and their
products with V. It also evaluates the same-word global Huber reference
1/(1+2sum h) from accepted b98bb807d514e80d. An exact closed formula for
the schedule sum, checked against the actual entries within the same job,
is sum h=2^m-1+a*(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).
Numerical products with rigorous benchmark bounds are still numerical
diagnostics; no floating-point computation becomes a rigorous proof.

Reported diagnostics include terminal F, full y/P, R2, Ltree, V,
2^m F, the terminal deepest-pair entropy 2^(1-m)logcosh(y_(m-1)),
the reference bounds, probability and state extrema, sign changes,
objective cancellation diagnostics, and complete physical query counts.
Finite successes or failures do not prove or refute an eventual statement.

Literature search: Matlas's covariance/diameter query returned unrelated
results. Blanchard--Higham--Higham's 2021 primary article
https://doi.org/10.1093/imanum/draa038 (arXiv:1909.03469) was read through
the browser for numerical background only. Direct publisher download
returned HTTP Error 403: Forbidden; no external theorem is imported.
All stable formulas needed for this pilot were derived above.

# Tree-Gibbs fixed-trajectory discovery: completed finite pilot

Status: NUMERICAL DISCOVERY ONLY. No verifier submission or new accepted fact.
The original unrestricted exact-profile target remains OPEN. The specified
eventual tree-Gibbs entropy inequality also remains undecided by this batch.

All three discovery trajectories are numerically far below the requested
benchmark scale. The independent calibration and precision checks passed.
The named ledger is CLOSED at five charged jobs and 20 accounted seconds.
Nothing in this report renews the model, numerical, or formal allocation.

## Frozen object, source, and raw outputs

The complete direct derivation is [PLAN.md](PLAN.md). It was published as
shared plan dddf7d2f6bbce301 BEFORE any mathematical evaluation. It defines
the actual leaf features, function, attained unique minimum, sharp global
smoothness, true radius, invariant spine, original physical word, group
probabilities, and the stable nested evaluator. These are source justifications
within a numerical pilot, not verified mathematical predecessors.

For reference, at enclosing depth m the unchanged definitions are

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c;
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m;
    n=2^m-1, r_tree=2/c, Zgeom=sum_(j<m)r_tree^j, w_j=r_tree^j/Zgeom;
    a_s=sum_(j<m)sqrt(w_j)(-1)^s_(j+1)e_(s_1...s_j);
    F(x)=log(2^-m sum_s exp(<a_s,x>));
    x_star=0, F_star=0, Ltree=(1+w_0)/2;
    x_0,(0^j)=log(2)/sqrt(w_j), all other coordinates zero;
    R2=(log 2)^2 r_tree^(1-m) Zgeom^2.

Every child in H_m retains the enclosing m. With y_j=sqrt(w_j)x_(0^j),
q_k=2^(-k-1) for k<m and q_m=2^-m, let

    ell_k=sum_(j<k)y_j-y_k, k<m; ell_m=sum_(j<m)y_j;
    P_k=q_k exp(ell_k)/sum_l q_l exp(ell_l);
    y'_j=y_j-(h_i*w_j/Ltree)(sum_(k>j)P_k-P_j).

The initial probabilities are 1/(m+4) on each side group and 4/(m+4)
on the all-left leaf. All n physical updates and the final query were
performed. No isolated child was restarted, and no alternative function,
initialization, feature geometry, or word was evaluated.

Frozen [evaluate.py](evaluate.py) SHA256:
`38f105fb1c508921585c93096ef0739ec80d0df7c7436da2a3070ca69b74e561`.
Frozen [run_job.py](run_job.py) SHA256:
`b9ee42af4a1a1729e55d1c74fc40dcee96a9353dae6f852263494732a8e4f053`.
The exact requests, tolerances, source hashes, and wall guards are in
[manifest.json](manifest.json) and each `job*.request.json`.
[artifact_integrity.json](artifact_integrity.json) records file hashes/sizes.

Complete raw query files, with every index 0 through n, are:

| Job | Physical steps | Raw query records | Output |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Full/reduced m2 calibration | 3 | 4 | [raw](job1_m2.trajectory.jsonl.gz), [summary with every full query](job1_m2.summary.json) |
| m6 discovery | 63 | 64 | [raw](job2_m6.trajectory.jsonl.gz), [summary](job2_m6.summary.json) |
| m10 discovery | 1023 | 1024 | [raw](job3_m10.trajectory.jsonl.gz), [summary](job3_m10.summary.json) |
| m14 discovery | 16383 | 16384 | [raw](job4_m14.trajectory.jsonl.gz), [summary](job4_m14.summary.json) |
| m10 Decimal60 independent check | 1023 | 1024 | [raw](job5_m10_precision.trajectory.jsonl.gz), [summary](job5_m10_precision.summary.json) |

Binary64 raw rows contain i,y,P,F,F_shifted,d,next_h, residuals and flags.
The actual full spine gradient is sqrt(w_j)d_j; off-spine components vanish
by the direct symmetry argument. Decimal rows retain all computed digits as
strings. The summaries' five checkpoints are explicitly SAMPLED, while the
compressed raw files are complete. The full final state is also in every
summary. No raw-output truncation or trajectory timeout occurred.

## Calibration and independent precision check

At m2, the full 4-leaf/3-coordinate GD and the independent reduced GD were
compared at every query 0,1,2,3. Maximum state, gradient, and group-probability
differences were each 1.1102230246251565e-16. Maximum objective difference
was 1.3530843112619095e-16. The full initial squared radius and the closed
formula both gave 2.096190037146931. Full minimum value/gradient were zero;
the quarter-diameter versus Ltree difference was 1.11e-16. All checks pass
the frozen 2e-12 tolerances. Shared calibration record: af041840350ebe0e.

The reserved fifth job rebuilt the m10 parameters and original word at
60 decimal digits. It used grouped exponentials and direct group-tail
derivatives, independently of the nested binary64 evaluation. It compared
ALL 1024 queries against the saved m10 trajectory. Maximum state difference
was 8.326672684688674e-16; maximum probability difference 1.11e-16;
maximum objective absolute difference 5.55e-17. Its terminal objective is

    0.000078336205125371097474550080359549856609888698127711152973

and the terminal relative discrepancy is 2.076055709011539e-15. The frozen
state/probability 1e-9 and terminal-objective relative 1e-8 checks pass.
Decimal reports Inexact/Rounded flags, as expected for transcendental
evaluation; there is no underflow or overflow. This agreement is NOT an
interval enclosure and does not substitute for a proof.

## Terminal values and the paid benchmark comparison

All entries below are numerical outputs, not exact rational equalities.
The instance score is V=2F(x_n)/(Ltree R2), in the project's W=2R convention.

| m | n | F(x_n) | R2 | Ltree | V |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 6 | 63 | 1.2221762704876316e-3 | 24.066031951812832 | 0.6662740285482042 | 1.524426428016792e-4 |
| 10 | 1023 | 7.833620512537125e-5 | 89.61232134567855 | 0.6311397309560824 | 2.770124425875675e-6 |
| 14 | 16383 | 4.744697075039196e-6 | 250.34461795633229 | 0.6165827433510951 | 6.147646132863358e-8 |

Accepted fact 04219554e41d9750, proof (7.1) and (9.3), supplies the closed
bounds 4lambda^(m-1)<=B_(2^m-1)<=4lambda^m. They were evaluated inside
the registered jobs; no new benchmark recurrence or proof replay was run.
Thus the following are numerical endpoints for the comparison with the
ACTUAL B_n, obtained from its rigorous enclosing reference formulas:

| m | Lower-reference product with V | Upper-reference product with V | V / same-word global Huber |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 6 | 0.05000862195983469 | 0.1207314933710219 | 0.06744312125458948 |
| 10 | 0.030870270579164635 | 0.0745274259063464 | 0.05269822296097517 |
| 14 | 0.023273021014856416 | 0.05618604297146041 | 0.045943954973704855 |

The target is B_n V>=1. Even the upper-reference products are far below1
in these finite numerical trajectories. These do not certify mathematical
counterexamples because the trajectory errors have not been rigorously
enclosed, and they do not refute an eventual statement. They do provide
strong finite discovery evidence against declaring this initialization a
benchmark-scale witness merely from positive entropy.

For the same reason, using B_n's upper bound gives numerical lower bounds
on the required final entropy of 0.01012309411871302 (m6),
0.0010511057395677544 (m10), and 0.00008444618670599841 (m14).
Compare these with the actual F column above. No exact B_n was computed.
The Huber column uses accepted b98bb807d514e80d's global term
1/(1+2sum h), not its entire K_n and not a full alternating-ray or complex
channel optimization. No new comparison to those stronger channels is claimed.

## Evolution diagnostics and analytical leads

The actual rescaled entropy 2^m F(x_n) is, respectively,
0.07821928131120842, 0.08021627404838017, 0.07773711687744218.
These finite values do not establish convergence or a positive limiting
lower bound. The last-coordinate values are 0.2677692822122471,
0.26515898991001385, 0.2593460809384506. The deepest-pair entropy terms
2^(1-m)logcosh(y_(n,m-1)) are 0.001107181223234944,
0.00006787159726180173, and 0.000004060037220444798.

In each of the three recorded complete trajectories, the first m-2 fields
change sign exactly once, the final two fields never change sign, and the
largest absolute field is the initial log2. These are finite diagnostics,
not a proposed proved invariant. At the terminal point shallow group
probabilities are close to their reference dyadic masses; the last few
coordinates retain the visible entropy. The precise full terminal fields
are:

    m6: [-0.0012395586350471754, -0.002545603234802957,
         -0.005542512047800676, -0.01683699394373122,
          0.0421163633704613, 0.2677692822122471]

    m10: [-7.836740611990384e-5, -0.00015683826183075385,
          -0.00031422760718545164, -0.0006314053768366603,
          -0.001278815164971219, -0.002647713488632224,
          -0.005861355598862788, -0.017319027378274936,

# Round56 handoff: one changed support geometry

All new mathematics is **UNVERIFIED**. No fact_submit was authorized or used.
The original exact-profile target remains open. This is the one assigned
analytical round after completed55; no renewal is requested or inferred.

Selected mechanism: a logarithmic-spiral gradient support curve, with
positive within-block and signed neighboring-block temporal correlations.
The proof supplies both a single global Moreau envelope with exact closest
minimizer and a source-format half-sum realization. Both are static finite
dimensional convex1-smooth functions; the full physical word and all
directed interpolation rows are retained, including zeros and large steps.

The direct source-format candidate is

    W_n(h) >= 1/[2(1+s_tail)]
      product_i [b_i/(c_sp(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i)]^2,
    b_i >= 2(1+epsilon^(-2)).

It uses the original source numerator, threshold and terminal factor.
For epsilon=kappa and matched horizontal coefficient c_sp, the explicit
new constant satisfies kappa log(a_sp)->pi/2. The published circular
sufficient constant instead has kappa log(a)/log(1/kappa)->2/pi.
The complete definitions and proofs are in spiral_checkpoint_geometry.md.

The exact finite equal-angle family also shows why the new cost is not
lossless. After paying the unused horizontal budget, a necessary additive
allowance E_sp satisfies kappa log(E_sp)->pi/2. A polynomial allowance
makes the original directed row Q_(end,star) strictly positive for an
actual finite word. This closes only that shortcut for the specified
zero-height reset geometry; it is not an upper bound on unrestricted W.

Remaining losses are explicit: c_sp per checkpoint, the activation
allowance, epsilon b for unbounded selected steps, the checkpoint cutoff
and terminal factor. The other realization uses b-1 and computes the
exact closest-minimizer radius. None of these estimates establishes a
uniform relative loss tending to1 against the original ALL-split B_n.

Shared records:

- Plan: 9e0d2abd4b36b2b3.
- Full joint proof: cdd7f057606aeb18.
- Finite original-row obstruction: 138b57940a77871b.
- Remaining target obligation: a4f6e6bcd81a2e12.

Primary source checked: Ye--Liu arXiv2609.09152v2, Section3.1 and full
AppendixA.1--A.3. Its Eq1 uses project W; its Eq40 retains the factor2
dropped from stated Lemma2.2. The stronger prefactor in PROBLEM.md is
therefore supported by the actual construction. No source statement was
promoted to a project fact.

Resources: zero mathematical computation, solvers, formal submissions,
installations, consults, or subagents. Two Matlas searches gave background
hits; the exact primary HTML was downloaded and read. Operational fixes
were an unavailable optional HTML parser, a malformed search pattern,
two patch-context failures and one trailing-newline archival mismatch.
The final proof evidence was read back byte-identically from shared GM;
the counterexample artifact was normalized to its shared evidence.

# Finite equal-angle obstruction to polynomial activation allowances
Status: UNVERIFIED companion to the joint spiral checkpoint candidate cdd7f057606aeb18.
No fact_submit or mathematical computation was used.

Targeted claim: for the specified spiral support geometry with a required zero
minimum at the old reset point, an inverse scale c_sp S+A(kappa), or
c_sp S+kappa b+A(kappa), can work for every ordinary word with a fixed
polynomially bounded nonnegative A(kappa) as kappa tends to0.
This claim is refuted by the following exact finite family, at draft level.

Fix 0<kappa<=1/2 and set epsilon=kappa,
Theta=arctan(1/kappa), c_sp=sqrt(1+kappa^2)exp(kappa Theta),
d_sp=1-kappa^2, T_sp=[exp(d_sp Theta/kappa)-1]/d_sp.
Put e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
e_t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta), R_sp(theta)=c_sp exp(-kappa theta),
v(theta)=R_sp(theta)e(theta), and K=conv({0} union {v(theta):0<=theta<=Theta}).
The vector n_sp(theta)=e(theta)+kappa e_t(theta) supports K at v(theta):
its inner product with v(phi), divided by R_sp(theta), is
exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+kappa sin Delta), Delta=phi-theta.
Its derivative is -(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta, so its maximum
on [-Theta,Theta] is1, at0. It is also strictly larger at v(theta) than at0.

For any integer M>=1 set Delta=Theta/M, theta_l=Theta-l Delta, and
A_D=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
B_D=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa,
t_0=0, t_l=B_D+A_D t_(l-1),
r_l=R_sp(theta_l)[(1+t_l)e(theta_l)+kappa t_l e_t(theta_l)],
alpha_l=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D+(1+kappa^2)B_D t_(l-1).

All t_l>=0 and alpha_l>=0. Resolving radial and tangential components
gives r_l-r_(l-1)=alpha_l v(theta_l). The supporting-normal calculation
gives Pi_K(r_l)=v(theta_l). Hence the ACTUAL finite chronological
ordinary word (alpha_M,...,alpha_1) follows these projection gradients
from r_M to r_0=(kappa,-1). Every step is finite; this family is defined
analytically, without executing any sweep.

Let S_M=sum alpha_l, P_M=(r_M)_1-kappa, Q_M=-(r_M)_2-1.
Then P_M=c_sp(1+t_M)-kappa and 1+Q_M=c_sp kappa t_M.
For sufficiently large M, A_D>1. Exact summation gives
t_M=B_D(A_D^M-1)/(A_D-1),
S_M=M a_D+(1+kappa^2)B_D(t_M-M B_D)/(A_D-1),
where a_D=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D.
The derivatives at Delta0 imply
t_M -> T_sp,
S_M -> (1+kappa^2)T_sp/d_sp-kappa(1+kappa^2)Theta/d_sp.

Now center and scale the finite support envelope to start at (1,0):
z_circ=(1,0)-sigma(kappa+P_M,-1-Q_M),
phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>,
where V_fin contains0, both endpoint vectors and every gradient just constructed.
The terminal local jet is
x_end=z_circ+sigma(kappa,-1),
g_end=sigma(kappa,-1),
f_end=sigma^2(1+kappa^2)/2.
It is an actual envelope jet, because (kappa,-1) belongs to K.

If the old reset point0 is to remain a minimizer with f_star=0,g_star=0,
the ORIGINAL directed interpolation row must satisfy
Q_(end,star)=f_end-<g_end,x_end>+||g_end||^2/2
 =sigma[-kappa+sigma(kappa(kappa+P_M)+1+Q_M)]<=0.
This is exactly the endpoint-plane condition phi(0)=0. It forces

    sigma^(-1)>=kappa+P_M+(1+Q_M)/kappa.

Thus the excess after paying the FULL available horizontal budget is
E_M=kappa+P_M+(1+Q_M)/kappa-c_sp S_M, and its exact limit is

    E_sp=c_sp[1+(1-3kappa^2)T_sp/d_sp
                       +kappa(1+kappa^2)Theta/d_sp]>0,
    lim_(kappa->0) kappa log E_sp=pi/2.

Therefore a universal inverse scale c_sp S+A(kappa) requires A(kappa)>=E_sp.
For the form c_sp S+kappa b+A(kappa), take the legal fixed local
checkpoint b=1+kappa^(-2); it requires A(kappa)>=E_sp-kappa b.
If A(kappa)<=C kappa^(-p) for any fixed finite C,p, choose kappa small
enough to violate this strict limiting inequality, then choose a finite
M large enough. The displayed ORIGINAL row Q_(end,star) is strictly positive.
The failed conclusion is the promised preserved minimum/interpolation
condition, not smoothness of the support envelope at a different minimum.

This obstruction keeps the actual finite chronological states and pays
c_sp S_M-P_M before declaring a deficit. It is stronger than merely
observing Q_M is large. It applies only to this support curve and the
specified zero-height reset architecture. Positive-height gluings,
other endpoints/curves, and unrestricted W_n remain open.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000589 -->
# A logarithmic-spiral checkpoint geometry and its paid activation obstruction

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical candidate. Worker max4, round56, under
TASK.md and master_guidance0682362f7b48ea2d. No numerical experiment,
symbolic evaluator, solver, benchmark computation, or fact_submit was used.
There is ONE selected replacement mechanism: change the gradient support
curve from a circular arc to a logarithmic spiral. The global function is
constructed below before its run. All new lemmas are parts of this one
candidate, not separately accepted facts.

## 1. Target, source audit, and the changed interface

For a finite nonnegative word h of length n, let W_n(h) be the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over finite-dimensional convex
differentiable L-smooth functions with an attained minimum and GD
x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t)/L. Thus W=2R for the project's R.

The original benchmark remains U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. For clarity its ALL-split
definition is C_0=B_0=1 and

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).

No recurrence or benchmark constant is modified in this draft.

Primary source: Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, *Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal
for Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.09152v2, September10,2026,
https://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2. The unversioned arXiv landing page
still identified v2 as current when read in this round. Exact HTML and its
extracted text are in this directory's downloads/. Read portions were
Section2, Section3.1, and the full proofs in AppendixA.1--A.3.

This paragraph is a source audit, not a project fact. The paper's Eq(1)
uses denominator (L/2)||x_0-x_star||^2, so its symbol R denotes project W.
Its stated Lemma2.2 retains only 1/[4(1+s_tail)]. But its actual Eq(40)
constructs a function with value D^2/[4(1+s_tail)] at initial distance1;
the paper explicitly drops the extra factor2 in passing to the lemma.
Thus the stronger prefactor 1/[2(1+s_tail)] in PROBLEM.md Section7 is
supported by the construction, despite the notation discrepancy.

The source takes

    c_Y=sqrt(1+2 gamma+epsilon^2),
    R_Y=sqrt(epsilon^2+(1+gamma)^2),
    H_Y=[c_Y(1+gamma+R_Y)/(epsilon(gamma+R_Y))]^(R_Y/gamma)-1,
    C_Y=epsilon+(H_Y+1)/epsilon.

Its local steps are half the physical steps. A sufficient source choice
is a>=2C_Y/c_Y, with selected physical steps b>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)).
The non-anytime recursion additionally asks a>=max{2,2B_0+2}.

There are two concrete costs. In AppendixA.1 Eq(34), the circle's normal
rotation telescopes with exponent R_Y/gamma; its endpoint-angle ratio
contains 1/epsilon. In AppendixA.2 Eq(35), vanishing before activation
requires a bound for P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon. The ENDPOINT support plane
alone forces this latter quantity; it is not only a loose norm estimate.
The source's half-sum gluing also pays its smoothness overlap and retains
epsilon b in each transfer denominator. The new curve changes the first
of these geometric costs. The second cost will be tested, not discarded.

Shared prior awareness b70f11135ec8688e and4ff405e904d7da62 described a
circular one-envelope relay and its minimum-reset barrier. Their claims
are not premises here: the needed assembly and radius arguments are
proved again below for the new curve. Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 fixes
the convention for all ordered interpolation residuals; Section5 below
checks those residuals directly. Accepted0e7e78056dc6258c rules out an
orthogonal-moment shortcut; the present gradients retain correlations.

## 2. Statement of the candidate checkpoint lemma

Fix 0<kappa<=epsilon<=1/2. Define

    Theta=arctan(1/epsilon),
    c_sp=sqrt(1+epsilon^2) exp(kappa Theta),
    d_sp=1-kappa^2,
    T_sp=[exp(d_sp Theta/kappa)-1]/d_sp,
    H_sp=c_sp[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]-1,
    A_sp=epsilon+(H_sp+1)/epsilon.                       (1)

All these quantities are independent of the word and its length.

Partition ANY prescribed finite nonnegative word, in its original order,
as

    (block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k),

where each ordinary block may be empty, s_i is its sum, and every selected
checkpoint satisfies b_i>=1+epsilon^(-2). The case k=0 is allowed.
For each i<k, Section3 constructs local vectors from the actual ordered
steps in block_i, with displacements P_i,Q_i satisfying

    0<=P_i<=c_sp s_i,  0<=Q_i<=H_sp,  P_i>=epsilon Q_i.

Set

    M_i=P_i+max{epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon},
    sigma_i=1/M_i,
    D_0=1,  D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1),
    ell_0=0,  ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i).            (2)

For k>=1 there is one explicitly specified finite max-affine J on
R^(k+1), and one function f=env_1 J, such that the complete physical GD
run starts at e_0 and has

    2f(x_n)=D_k^2/(1+2s_k),
    min f=0,
    dist(e_0,argmin f)^2
      =sigma_0^2[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2].           (3)

Consequently this exact true-distance payoff is

    W_n(h) >= (b_0-1)^2
               /{[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2](1+2s_k)}
             times product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/M_i]^2. (4)

In particular the following fully explicit mass-only bound holds:

    W_n(h) >= 1/(1+2s_k)
      times product_(i=0)^(k-1)
        [(b_i-1)/(c_sp s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp})]^2. (5)

For k=0 use the one-dimensional Huber envelope, giving exactly
1/(1+2s_0). Multiplying the displayed functions by L gives the same
normalized payoffs and the prescribed steps h_t/L for every L>0.

The lemma covers every word and every checkpoint selection satisfying
its displayed condition. Zeros and arbitrarily large ordinary steps are
included. No cap is imposed on selected steps. Maximizing (4) or (5)
over allowed selections, and including k=0, is an explicit all-word
lower-bound channel; no comparison with B_n is assumed.

There is also a direct source-format corollary, proved in Section6A:
with a_sp=2A_sp/c_sp and checkpoints b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)),

    W_n(h)>=1/[2(1+s_k)]
        times product_(i=0)^(k-1)
          [b_i/(c_sp(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i)]^2.       (5a)

It preserves the source's numerator, terminal factor and threshold,
while replacing its certified auxiliary constant. Thus the improvement
is not obtained by concealing the b_i-1 cost of the separate formula(5).

## 3. The new support curve and a backward construction for arbitrary steps

For 0<=theta<=Theta define

    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
    e_t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),
    R_sp(theta)=c_sp exp(-kappa theta),
    v(theta)=R_sp(theta)e(theta),
    n_sp(theta)=e(theta)+kappa e_t(theta).

Let K be the convex hull of 0 and this compact arc. Write
v(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)). The endpoint values are
v(0)=(c_sp,0), v(Theta)=(epsilon,-1). Differentiating gives

    p'=-R_sp(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,
    q'= R_sp(cos theta-kappa sin theta)>=0.

The second inequality uses kappa<=epsilon=cot Theta. Hence

    epsilon<=p<=c_sp, 0<=q<=1, q/p<=1/epsilon.         (6)

For delta=phi-theta in [-Theta,Theta],

    <n_sp(theta),v(phi)>/R_sp(theta)
       =exp(-kappa delta)(cos delta+kappa sin delta).

The derivative of the right side in delta is
-(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa delta)sin delta. Its maximum on this interval is
therefore1, at delta0. Since <n_sp(theta),0>=0<R_sp(theta), n_sp(theta)
is an outward supporting normal to K at v(theta). At v(0), (0,1) is
also a supporting normal because every point of K has second coordinate
nonpositive. This proves all support inequalities directly; no theorem
about spiral convexity is imported.

Now let alpha_1,...,alpha_m be any finite nonnegative ordinary block.
Set r_m=v_m=v(Theta) and work backward. Given r_j=(u_j,-y_j) and
alpha=alpha_j, make the following choice.

If

    y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)c_sp],                     (7)

put v_(j-1)=v(0), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(0).
Otherwise choose the unique theta in (0,theta_j] satisfying

    <r_j,e(theta)-kappa^(-1)e_t(theta)>/R_sp(theta)
          =1-alpha,                                 (8)

put v_(j-1)=v(theta), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(theta).
Here theta_j is the angle of v_j. Once a capped vector v(0) occurs,
(7) applies at every subsequent backward step, so (8) is only needed
when theta_j>0. Equation(8) defines a number uniquely; it is not an
oracle, numerical feasibility assumption, or executed solver.

We prove by backward induction that these rules are well-defined, that
v_j is the Euclidean projection of r_j on K, and that

    u_j>=epsilon, y_j>=1, y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon.          (9)

For a noncapped point the stronger representation is

    r_j=R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)e(theta_j)
                            +kappa t_j e_t(theta_j)],
    t_j>=0.                                         (10)

It holds at j=m with theta_m=Theta,t_m=0. For a capped step let
mu=u_j+(alpha-1)c_sp. Condition(7) implies mu>=y_j/kappa>0, and

    r_(j-1)-v(0)=mu n_sp(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j).

This is a nonnegative sum of the two supporting normals already checked,
so it certifies projection. It also yields
y_(j-1)<=kappa(u_(j-1)-c_sp), which implies(7) at every earlier step.

If(7) fails, the current point is not capped. By(10), its polar angle
is at least theta_j and the left side of(8) equals1 at theta_j.
At theta0 the left side is (u_j-y_j/kappa)/c_sp<1-alpha.
Its derivative is

    (kappa+kappa^(-1))(u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)
       /R_sp(theta)>0.

Thus there is exactly one root theta in(0,theta_j]. The tangential
component <r_j,e_t(theta)> is nonnegative. Equation(8) says exactly
that r_j+(alpha-1)v(theta) is a nonnegative multiple of n_sp(theta).
Projection and(10) follow from the supporting inequality. Adding alpha
times a vector obeying(6) preserves u>=epsilon,y>=1; the ratio y/u is
a convex combination of its previous value and q/p, giving(9).
This completes the induction, including alpha=0.

Define

    P=sum_(j=1)^m alpha_j p(theta_(j-1)),
    Q=sum_(j=1)^m alpha_j q(theta_(j-1)), S=sum alpha_j.

Then r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q). Bounds P<=c_sp S and P>=epsilon Q follow
from(6). It remains to prove the word-independent bound for Q.

Consider consecutive noncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new and put
Delta=theta_new-theta_old. Taking the tangential component in the exact
GD identity, and using(10), gives

    t_old=B(Delta)+A(Delta)t_new,
    A(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa.          (11)

For 0<=Delta<=Theta<pi/2 both A,B are nonnegative. Put d=d_sp.
Direct differentiation yields

    A'-(d/kappa)A
       =-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0.

Since A(0)=1, A<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Also B<=Delta/kappa, so
1+dB<=1+d Delta/kappa<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Therefore(11) implies

    1+d t_old<=exp(d Delta/kappa)(1+d t_new).

Telescoping from theta_m=Theta,t_m=0 proves

    t_j<=[exp(d(Theta-theta_j)/kappa)-1]/d<=T_sp.      (12)

At each noncapped point(10) now gives

    y_j=R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)sin theta_j
                              +kappa t_j cos theta_j]
       <=c_sp[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]=1+H_sp.

A capped backward step leaves y unchanged, so the same bound holds at
all capped points. Hence Q=y_0-1<=H_sp. Every inequality is uniform in
m and in all finite values of the alpha_j; no maximum stepsize was used.

## 4. Local finite planes and their exact zero/proximal regions

For one block suppress its index. Set

    A_loc=epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon,
    M=P+max{epsilon b,A_loc}, sigma=1/M,
    z_circ=(1,0)-sigma(epsilon+P,-1-Q),
    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon b)>=0, Y=sigma Q.

Let V_fin consist of0,v(0),v_0,...,v_m and define

    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>.         (13)

All objects are fixed by the supplied block and b. The smaller convex
hull conv(V_fin) contains every v_j and is contained in K; thus the
projection inequalities proved in Section3 still hold on it. At

    x_j=z_circ+sigma r_j, z_j=x_j-sigma v_j,

the plane with slope sigma v_j is active in phi, giving
sigma v_j in partial phi(z_j). Consequently z_j is the proximal point
of phi at x_j, and grad env_1 phi(x_j)=sigma v_j.

Every nonzero v=(p,-q) obeys p>=0,q>=0,q<=p/epsilon. At the origin,

    <v,-z_circ>
     =-p+sigma[p(epsilon+P)+q(1+Q)]
     <=-p+sigma p(P+A_loc)<=0.

Thus phi=0 on {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}. At(U,Y),

    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>
       =sigma[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0                    (14)

because b>=1+epsilon^(-2). Increasing its second argument preserves
this inequality, so phi(U,y)=0 for y>=Y.

The local GD path starts at(1,0). Its ordinary block ends at
(U+sigma epsilon b,Y), with gradient sigma(epsilon,-1); checkpoint b
then reaches (U,Y+sigma b). Every ordinary/checkpoint-query proximal
point has first coordinate at least U and second coordinate in
[0,sigma(1+Q)]. To check the first claim, the normal representations give
u_j>=p_j: for an interior point this follows from(10), since
cos theta-kappa sin theta>=0, and for a capped point it follows from
the normal representation. Hence

    (z_j)_1-U=sigma[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]>=0.

For the second claim, (z_j)_2=sigma[1+Q-y_j+q_j]. The inequalities
1<=y_j<=1+Q and0<=q_j<=1 give the two endpoints.

These statements concern the nonsmooth planes, precisely where the
global maximum in the next section must be tested.

## 5. One static global function and ALL directed interpolation rows

Use coordinates0,...,k and quantities(2). For i<k put

    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i).

Set delta_tail=D_k/(1+2s_k),

    Phi_k(z)=delta_tail max{z_k-ell_k,0},
    J(z)=max_{0<=i<=k} Phi_i(z),
    f(x)=min_z[J(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                     (15)

This is a finite maximum of specified affine planes, including the zero
plane. It is nonnegative and J(0)=0 by Section4 and ell_i>=0. The
quadratic term makes the proximal minimizer unique and attained.

For completeness, the minimizing equation is x-z in partial J(z).
The two subgradient inequalities at z,z' give
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'> for g=x-z,g'=x'-z'. For an increment w,
using z as a trial proximal point gives
f(x+w)<=f(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2. If z' is the proximal point at x+w,
the inequality J(z')>=J(z)+<g,z'-z> gives, by expansion,
f(x+w)>=f(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2. Consequently
0<=f(x+w)-f(x)-<g,w><=||w||^2/2. This proves differentiability,
the supporting inequalities prove convexity, and the preceding
monotonicity estimate proves1-smoothness. It has minimum0 and is
everywhere finite. No external interpolation theorem is required.

We verify the WHOLE actual trajectory. At the start of block i the
claimed coordinates are frozen x_j=ell_j+D_j U_j for j<i, current
coordinate x_i=ell_i+D_i, and all coordinates j>i equal0. Use the
Section4 trajectory in coordinates i,i+1. At each of its candidate
GLOBAL proximal points:

* the current component Phi_i has the prescribed subgradient;
* component i-1 has first input U_(i-1) and second input at least
  ell_i/D_(i-1)>=Y_(i-1), so is0 by(14);
* every earlier component is still in the same zero region;
* component i+1 has first input at most0, because the second proximal
  coordinate is at most D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i)=ell_(i+1), and second input0;
* every later component is also in its pre-activation zero region.

The terminal hinge is included in this last check. Thus Phi_i dominates
every other component at the candidate proximal point, even if all
values there are0. Its prescribed subgradient is a subgradient of J,
proving the actual global gradient. After the checkpoint, coordinate
i freezes at ell_i+D_i U_i, and coordinate i+1 equals

    D_i(Y_i+sigma_i b_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1).

This proves the induction, without removing any old or future plane.

During the terminal block, after accumulated steps u in[0,s_k], the
moving coordinate is ell_k+D_k-u delta_tail. Its proximal coordinate
is at least ell_k because
D_k-(u+1)delta_tail>=0. All earlier pieces remain0 there. The actual
gradient is consequently delta_tail e_k at every terminal-block query
and at x_n. Evaluation gives

    f(x_n)=delta_tail D_k-(s_k+1/2)delta_tail^2
           =D_k^2/[2(1+2s_k)].                        (16)

Here is also an exact check of the original ALL-directed constraints.
At every sample, including the unqueried terminal, put g_i=grad f(x_i)
and z_i=x_i-g_i. The preceding construction proves
g_i in partial J(z_i) and f_i=J(z_i)+||g_i||^2/2. For every ordered
pair of samples, direct cancellation yields

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =J(z_i)-J(z_j)+<g_i,z_j-z_i><=0.              (17)

The same equation applies when either sample is any minimizer, with
g_star=0,J(z_star)=0. Thus all remote, prefix, terminal, and optimum
rows have been paid. Zero steps give identical actual jets and also
satisfy(17); no formal coordinate deletion is used to assert legality.

The Gram matrix is the Gram matrix of these explicit real vectors in
R^(k+1), hence is positive semidefinite. Within a block the gradient
inner products are positive multiples of cos(theta_i-theta_j)>0.
Neighboring components share one coordinate and can have negative
inner products. The construction is deterministic and retains these
temporal correlations; it averages no oracle branches.

## 6. The closest minimizer and the exact normalization

For k>=1 define x_star with first two coordinates

    (x_star)_0=1-sigma_0(epsilon+P_0),
    (x_star)_1=sigma_0(1+Q_0)=ell_1,

and all later coordinates0. The first component is evaluated at its
center, the next has first input0, and all later pieces have nonpositive
first input and zero second input. Thus J(x_star)=0. Also argmin f
equals {J=0}, because both terms in(15) are nonnegative and attained.

Every first-component plane is active with value0 at x_star. Moreover

    e_0-x_star
      =[(P_0-epsilon Q_0)/c_sp] sigma_0(c_sp,0,0,...)
       +(1+Q_0) sigma_0(epsilon,-1,0,...).

Both coefficients are nonnegative. Each displayed slope has nonpositive
inner product with z-x_star for every z satisfying J(z)=0. Hence
<e_0-x_star,z-x_star><=0. Expanding ||e_0-z||^2 proves that x_star is
the closest minimizer. The squared radius is exactly(3), and dividing
(16) by it cancels sigma_0^2 to give(4).

Since epsilon<=1,

    sqrt((epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2)
       <=P_0+epsilon+(1+Q_0)/epsilon<=M_0.

Also M_i<=c_sp s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}. These inequalities prove(5).
For k=0, J(z)=delta max{z,0} with delta=1/(1+2s_0) has closest
minimizer0 to initial1 and gives the stated equality. All dimensions,
minimum values, and distance factors are therefore explicit.

## 6A. The same improved geometry in the original checkpoint formula

This is a second realization of the SAME spiral curve, to compare its
auxiliary allowance with the source without changing other factors.
It uses a half-sum of local envelopes, as in the audited source. Here
the specified genuine minimizer is0 and the actual initial norm about
that minimizer is1. Formula(4) above separately supplies an exact
closest-minimizer result for the one-envelope realization.

Choose a_sp>=2A_sp/c_sp. For each block i run Section3 on its ordinary
steps divided by2, and set S=s_i/2, b=b_i/2 and A=c_sp a_sp/2. In this
paragraph only, choose

    sigma=1/(c_sp S+epsilon b+A)

in(13). This possibly smaller scale still satisfies both inequalities
sigma(P+epsilon b)<=1 and sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1, since
P<=c_sp S and A>=A_sp. All local gradient and zero-region proofs in
Section4 remain valid. Write f_i=env_1 phi_i, with its resulting U_i,Y_i.
Its local checkpoint transfer is sigma b, namely

    G_i=b_i/[c_sp(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i].

For this realization use fresh lengths D'_0=1,
D'_(i+1)=D'_i G_i and thresholds ell'_0=0,
ell'_(i+1)=D'_i Y_i. Define

    F(x)=(1/2)sum_(i<k) (D'_i)^2
             f_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)
          +(1/2)H_delta(x_k-ell'_k),
    delta=D'_k/(1+s_k),
    H_delta(z)=0 (z<=0), z^2/2 (0<=z<=delta),
               delta z-delta^2/2 (z>=delta).         (18a)

Each local f_i is convex and1-smooth by the envelope proof in Section5.
Each coordinate appears in at most two local terms, counting the
terminal Huber term. Summing their quadratic upper bounds, with weight
1/2, gives F(x+w)<=F(x)+<grad F(x),w>+||w||^2/2. This implies the
gradient is1-Lipschitz: apply that upper inequality to the convex
function F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x and a unit negative-gradient step.
Since its minimum is at y, the result is
F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.
Add the exchanged inequality and use Cauchy--Schwarz.

The same local zero regions show F>=0,F(0)=0. At the beginning of
block i the frozen coordinates are ell'_j+D'_j U_j for j<i, the current
coordinate is ell'_i+D'_i, and all later coordinates are0. During its
local trajectory, coordinate i never falls below ell'_i, and coordinate
i+1 increases from0 to D'_i Y_i before the checkpoint. Hence every old
component stays in its post-checkpoint zero region and every future
component stays in its pre-activation zero region. Only component i
has nonzero gradient, and its factor1/2 converts each physical step to
the chosen half step. The checkpoint gives the next stated starting
state. This verifies the entire word for the ONE function(18a).

In the terminal block the gradient is delta/2, and the final coordinate
above ell'_k is D'_k-delta s_k/2>=delta. Direct substitution into the
last Huber term gives F(x_n)=(D'_k)^2/[4(1+s_k)]. Initial x_0=e_0 and
the specified minimizer0 have squared distance exactly1. Therefore
W_n(h)>=2F(x_n), proving(5a). For k=0 the same last Huber term proves
the empty product. The all-directed Q_ij<=0 follow from the smooth
convex inequality just proved, including the actual terminal and0.
The dimension is k+1 and all entries, including zeros, remain in the
physical run.

## 7. Quantitative improvement and an exact finite-word obstruction

First compare certified auxiliary costs at the SAME horizontal
coefficient. Take epsilon=kappa tending to0, retain c_sp from(1), and
in the circular source choose

    gamma_match=[c_sp^2-1-kappa^2]/2>0.

Then c_Y=c_sp, so this does not exchange the gain for a larger linear
coefficient of S. Elementary logarithmic limits in the displayed
formulas give

    lim_(kappa->0) kappa log A_sp=pi/2,
    lim_(kappa->0) kappa log(H_Y+1)/log(1/kappa)=2/pi. (18)

For the second limit, gamma_match/kappa->pi/2, R_Y->1, and the base
inside H_Y is asymptotic to2/kappa. For the first, log A_sp differs
from d_sp Theta/kappa by terms whose product with kappa tends0.
These are parameter asymptotics for explicit constants, not an o(1)
loss asserted in the target theorem. In particular A_sp/C_Y tends0.
The new certified cost removes the factor log(1/kappa) in the exponent
of the published circular allowance.

For the direct corollary(5a), a_sp=2A_sp/c_sp has the same limit
kappa log a_sp->pi/2. Its ratio to the source's sufficient choice
2C_Y/c_sp tends0. If the extra polynomial floors used by the source's
non-anytime recursion are imposed on both choices, these parameter
asymptotics do not change. This draft does not import or reprove that
recursion's resulting exponent theorem.

That improvement is not enough to make this geometry lossless. The
following finite family tests the ACTUAL activation budget, including
the slack c_sp S-P, rather than merely bounding Q.

Fix the parameters and an integer M>=1. Set Delta=Theta/M,
theta_l=Theta-l Delta for0<=l<=M, and define

    A_D=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B_D=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa,
    t_0=0, t_l=B_D+A_D t_(l-1),
    r_l=R_sp(theta_l)[(1+t_l)e(theta_l)
                                   +kappa t_l e_t(theta_l)],
    alpha_l=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D
              +(1+kappa^2)B_D t_(l-1).              (19)

Every alpha_l is nonnegative. Tangential and radial components show
exactly that r_l-r_(l-1)=alpha_l v(theta_l). The normal inequality
from Section3 proves v(theta_l)=Pi_K(r_l). Thus the finite chronological
word (alpha_M,...,alpha_1), started at r_M, follows these states back
to r_0=(epsilon,-1). This is an exact family for every M, not an
executed sweep or a replacement by continuous-time dynamics.

Write S_M=sum alpha_l, P_M=(r_M)_1-epsilon,
Q_M=-(r_M)_2-1. Since theta_M=0,

    P_M=c_sp(1+t_M)-epsilon,
    1+Q_M=c_sp kappa t_M.                            (20)

For all sufficiently large M, A_D>1. The elementary geometric sum and
the recursion in(19) give

    t_M=B_D(A_D^M-1)/(A_D-1),
    S_M=M a_D+(1+kappa^2)B_D(t_M-MB_D)/(A_D-1),
    a_D=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D.

Using derivatives at Delta0, or the defining limits of exp, gives

    t_M -> T_sp,
    S_M -> (1+kappa^2)T_sp/d_sp
                 -kappa(1+kappa^2)Theta/d_sp.         (21)

The scaled endpoint plane at the origin in(13) imposes NECESSARILY

    sigma^(-1)>=epsilon+P+(1+Q)/epsilon.              (22)

Indeed substitute v(Theta)=(epsilon,-1) into phi(0)=0 to obtain it.
For the equal-angle family the additive excess left AFTER paying the
linear budget c_sp S_M is therefore

    E_M=epsilon+P_M+(1+Q_M)/epsilon-c_sp S_M
       -> E_sp
        =c_sp[1+(kappa/epsilon-2kappa^2/d_sp)T_sp
                       +kappa(1+kappa^2)Theta/d_sp]. (23)

When epsilon=kappa<=1/2 this becomes

    E_sp=c_sp[1+(1-3kappa^2)T_sp/d_sp
                       +kappa(1+kappa^2)Theta/d_sp]>0,
    lim_(kappa->0) kappa log E_sp=pi/2.               (24)

More precisely kappa A_sp/E_sp tends1 in this diagonal regime. The
upper allowance and this finite-family necessary allowance therefore
have the same exponential scale, with their displayed bounds differing
by a factor asymptotic to1/kappa. This is a statement about geometric
parameter costs only.

For each fixed kappa, convergence means that actual finite words force
E_M arbitrarily close to E_sp. Thus any universal additive allowance
A with inverse scale c_sp S+A and the SAME endpoint/minimum-reset
zero condition must satisfy A>=E_sp. If its denominator also contains
epsilon b, take the legal fixed checkpoint b=1+kappa^(-2); necessarily
A>=E_sp-kappa b, which has the same exponential parameter scale.
This is a quantitative obstruction to a polynomial additive activation
allowance in this new family. It does not infer an obstruction from Q
alone or forget the available horizontal slack.

The scope of(22)--(24) is the specified spiral support geometry with
its actual endpoint plane and a common zero region. It says nothing
against positive-height global gluings, changed endpoints, or other
support curves. It does not upper-bound W_n for these words.

## 8. Every paid loss and the exact remaining target obligation

There are no hidden dimension or PSD losses: dimension is k+1, the
function is fixed before the run, and(17) is exact. Formula(4) uses the
true closest minimizer. Passing from(4) to(5) pays only the explicitly
displayed displacement bounds and the first-factor norm bound.

The remaining costs in(5) are:

* the checkpoint restriction b_i>=1+epsilon^(-2);
* the exact numerator b_i-1, caused by the future-plane proximal buffer;
* the coefficient c_sp>1 of every s_i;
* max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}, including unbounded selected steps;
* the terminal Huber denominator1+2s_k, retained exactly.

In(5a) the buffer and numerator loss are replaced by the half-sum
construction: the threshold is doubled, the numerator is b_i, the
denominator is c_sp(s_i+a_sp)+epsilon b_i, and the terminal factor is
1/[2(1+s_k)]. Relative to the product with denominator s_i and the
SAME terminal factor, its exact logarithmic loss, when all s_i>0, is

    Loss_source=2sum_i log[c_sp+(c_sp a_sp+epsilon b_i)/s_i].

Thus it too retains at least2k log c_sp and an explicit, unbounded-in-b
term. There is no assertion that its remaining multiplicative loss
tends1 uniformly over words.

For partitions with all s_i>0, compare(5) only as a DIAGNOSTIC with the
formal product I=product(b_i/s_i)^2/(1+2s_k). The logarithm of I divided
by the right side of(5) is exactly

    Loss=2sum_i log[c_sp+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}/s_i]
            -2sum_i log(1-1/b_i).                   (25)

In particular Loss>=2k log c_sp. Also

    Loss<=2k(c_sp-1)
        +2sum_i max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}/s_i
        +2sum_i 1/(b_i-1),                          (26)

by log(1+x)<=x and -log(1-u)<=u/(1-u). These explicit sums would have
to tend0 to use this sufficient near-unit comparison. They do not do
so uniformly over all words: ordinary blocks can have sum0, and
epsilon b_i/s_i is unbounded. Formula(5) itself remains defined and
valid when s_i=0; the diagnostic I is then simply not used. Neither
(25) nor(26) asserts that I is the original benchmark.

The actual outstanding task is to prove, with a threshold independent
of h, that an explicit legal adversary channel (this one, a stronger
one, or a combination with other accepted channels) reaches
(1-delta)/B_n for every delta>0 and every sufficiently large n and
every h in[0,infinity)^n. For this particular channel a concrete next
question is whether optimizing its displayed exact payoff(4), including
the Huber empty selection, controls the ORIGINAL all-split B_n with
that uniform relative error. This is a restricted, explicitly defined
family, not a restatement of unrestricted minimax feasibility.

No such comparison is proved here. The new support curve gives a
quantitatively smaller certified checkpoint cost, but its exact finite
obstruction(23) shows why sending its parameters to zero alone cannot
be presented as a lossless repair. No original-target completion,
new accepted fact, or formal verification is claimed.

# Frozen interface and numerical method

Allocation ddd2a22025cdbaac; model/effort supplied as gpt-6-astra / ultra.
This text is written before any numerical import or mathematical evaluation.
All mathematical conclusions remain unverified; this is numerical discovery.

Write P_i=sum_(k<i) h_k v_k and F_i=<z0,v_i>. The normal at z0-P_i
against v_j is <z0-P_i-v_i,v_j-v_i><=0. Expanding gives exactly
F_j-F_i<=<P_i+v_i,v_j-v_i>=C_ij. With Gram_ij=<v_i,v_j> and
A_ij=sum_(k<i)h_k Gram_kj, this is C_ij=A_ij-A_ii+Gram_ij-Gram_ii.
All original ordered pairs are required, including the terminal vertex.
There is no physical step h_n. For the zero vertex the normal is
-F_i+<P_i+v_i,v_i><=0, so a common shift of all F_i pays these finite
lower bounds. The full positive Walsh dictionary and positive radii make
the nonzero vertices independent, so every real vector F is represented by
one actual z0. Thus an original negative cycle excludes every initializer
for this fixed itinerary, not just a constant-blend value law.

Summing F_next-F_current<=C_current,next around a directed cycle gives
0<=sum C_current,next. Conversely, if there is no negative cycle, append
a zero-weight source to every vertex. Every walk can have its nonnegative
cycles deleted; finitely many simple paths attain shortest distances.
Appending any edge to a shortest path proves d_j<=d_i+C_ij. This is a
general exact finite-graph alternative, not a full-depth sign theorem.

The child uses ONE synchronous Bellman-Ford/min-plus run. Starting with
d_i=0, in each round it finds min_i(d_i+C_ij), then retains d_j if no
strict raw floating-point improvement occurs. At most N rounds run.
Each strict improvement records the incoming original edge. No tolerance
is inserted into relaxation, no edge is dropped, and C is never repaired.
If a round has no changes, the full original residual F_j-F_i-C_ij is
reported. After N changing rounds, ONE deterministic predecessor chain
from the smallest changed vertex is followed N times; its directed cycle
is reconstructed, and the original raw weights are summed in the child.
Numerical predecessor anomalies or a nonnegative reconstructed sum remain
unresolved defects rather than triggering a second extraction or retry.

Frozen reporting tolerance: machine epsilon e for binary64; S=max(1,max|C|);
t_edge=256*e*N*S; t_cycle=cycle_edge_count*t_edge. These are reporting
thresholds, not proved roundoff bounds. A cycle with fsum(weights)<-t_cycle
is robustly negative numerically; a raw negative sum within the threshold
is unresolved. A no-cycle-detected candidate requires the maximum ORIGINAL
residual<=t_edge. It is not an exact potential or a legal GD lower witness.
Raw fsum, ordinary sum, numpy sum, individual edge weights and residuals
are retained even when they disagree with a qualitative classification.

The six frozen jobs are the two prescribed fixtures and depths 6,8,9,10.
All four depths use enclosing word parameter M=1536, but dictionary N=2^depth
changes the radial damping and mixed-character weights. They are FINITE
ANALOGUES, not restrictions of N=2^1536 geometry and not depth>=1536.
There is no payoff, radius, blend, stepsize, SDP or benchmark optimization.

The stdlib broker charges all ten seconds and one graph-analysis slot before
launching each child through the existing public mathematical launcher.
Each child runs in a new process group, with all imports, construction,
relaxation, cycle extraction, array saves and numerical diagnostics inside
an externally enforced ten-second deadline. No numerical code runs in the
broker. Calibration failure closes the allocation and retires remaining
credit; every failure counts. Source and case hashes are frozen first.
After close, only saved-text transcription, administrative reads and byte
hashing are allowed. No mathematical extraction or automatic renewal.

Project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`; NEW allocation `1cc36024013ccc74`.

## Exact scope and frozen interface

For each fixed independent-vertex family, the frozen pre-execution derivation
sets P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k v_k and F_i=<z0,v_i>, and expands ALL nonzero-vertex
normal conditions into

    F_j-F_i <= C_ij,
    C_ij = <P_i+v_i,v_j-v_i>
         = A_ij-A_ii+Gram_ij-Gram_ii,
    A_ij = sum_(k<i)h_k Gram_kj.

Common shifts of the entire F vector preserve these constraints and can pay
the finite zero-vertex lower bounds. The full positive Walsh dictionary
makes the nonzero vertices independent. Thus an exact original negative
cycle would obstruct every initializer for this fixed itinerary, without a
constant-blend restriction. Conversely, the saved floating potentials do
not establish exact legality. The general finite graph alternative in the
frozen derivation is not a statement about the sign of any full-depth cycle.

Every production case uses the same enclosing word parameter M=1536, with
epsilon=1/(4M), beta=1-epsilon, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c and the
original positive steps h_i=1+a*c^nu2(i+1). The dictionary has CASE depth
j and N=2^j vertices. Its eta, radial damping and tiny mixed-character
weights depend on this finite N. These are FINITE ANALOGUES, NOT restrictions
of the full N=2^1536 geometry, NOT physical depth>=1536, and NOT instances
under the earlier full-depth hypotheses.

The frozen construction retains every mixed-character term, every original
ordered pair, and terminal vertex n=N-1. There is no physical h_n. It uses
the prescribed positive roots for diagnostics and the prescribed radial
ratios r0=rho0*(1-4/N), r1=rho1*(1-4/N), and r_l=1 at all higher levels.
No steps, roots, Gram matrix or C entries were repaired, clipped, averaged,
symmetrized or changed. Eigenvalues were not computed or repaired.

One synchronous Bellman-Ford/min-plus analysis per case starts at zero and
uses strict RAW improvements, without a tolerance in relaxation. Every
round minimizes over all original incoming edges, with a maximum N rounds.
Reporting tolerance was frozen as

    t_edge = 256 * binary64_epsilon * N * max(1,max(abs(C))),
    t_cycle = cycle_edge_count * t_edge.

These thresholds are not rigorous roundoff bounds. No detected cycle,
robust numerical negativity, tiny unresolved negativity and timeout remain
distinct statuses. In this batch the negative fixture has the robust
numerical status; production has the no-cycle-detected candidate status.
No production cycle was extracted because each run converged earlier.

No initializer z0 or common zero-row shift was reconstructed; no terminal
payoff, radius, lambda/alpha, stepsize or parameter optimization ran. There
was no SDP or benchmark dynamic program. The saved candidate potentials do
not prove that the finite model is exactly feasible, do not exclude negative
cycles missed through floating-point effects, and do not extend to arbitrary
full-depth values. No minimax lower bound or asymptotic separation is claimed.

# ONE frozen numerical-discovery batch: all-value Gray feasibility via original cycles

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. ONE fresh native invocation, model gpt-6-astra, reasoning ultra, consultOFF. ONE frozen batch, at most SIX charged mathematical child jobs, TEN external hard-timeout seconds each, total SIXTY accounted seconds. At most one complete graph-feasibility analysis per case. No retries, corrections, extra cases or automatic renewal. Two mandatory calibration jobs precede four prescribed discovery jobs; either calibration failure closes the batch and retires unused credit. Formal calls/fact_submit, installations, consults and subagents: ZERO. All older grants are CLOSED and unavailable.

Read OPERATOR.md, full PROBLEM.md, this TASK and exact budget.json. Read the FULL public source research/direct_gray_free_value_level_20261003/RESULT.md (21655 bytes, SHA2562db2648e2e401fd53bbcea1ca3634cd04851609bd81601d9d9957349e667b52c), plus its HANDOFF. The midpoint and two blend bands are excluded there in an UNVERIFIED draft, but arbitrary relative values and vertical initializers are NOT excluded. This is not another lambda scan.

Mathematical interface, to derive briefly as text BEFORE freezing code: given one fixed independent-vertex family v_i and actual cumulative physical displacement P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k v_k, the unknown initial inner products F_i=<z0,v_i> must satisfy
 F_j-F_i <= C_ij, C_ij=<P_i+v_i,v_j-v_i>, for EVERY original i,j.
The nonzero vertices are independent in the full Walsh dictionary, so any real F vector has a unique actual z0. Simultaneous nonzero-vertex normals are equivalent to these difference constraints. Adding the SAME scalar to every F_i preserves them and can pay every zero-vertex lower bound F_i>=<P_i+v_i,v_i>. Thus a negative directed cycle in the ORIGINAL C array rules out EVERY initial point and EVERY relative value choice for this fixed gradient itinerary, independently of constant-blend restrictions. Conversely a numerical potential with small residual is only a numerical candidate, not a proof. A general graph alternative may be proved from path sums, but no full-depth sign conclusion follows from finite data.

The SIX cases are fixed in this order:
1. Feasible directed-constraint fixture C=[[0,2,3],[0,0,-1],[5,3,0]], whose explicit potential F=(0,1,-1) is feasible.
2. The same fixture with C01=-3 instead of2. The original two-edge cycle0->1->0 has total-3. This must be detected with orientation correct. These two fixtures are graph checks, not GD certificates.
3-6. Physical/dictionary depths j=6,8,9,10, always using ONE enclosing M=1536 for the word parameters eps=1/(4M), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps), a=beta/c. Set N=2^j, n=N-1, actual h_i=1+a*c^nu2(i+1), i<n. The dictionary has j bits and N vertices. This is a FINITE ANALOGUE: changing dictionary N changes its radial damping and tiny-character weights; it is NOT the restriction of the full N=2^1536 geometry, NOT physical depth>=1536, and NOT a theorem under the old full-depth hypotheses. Preserve this limitation in every report.

For each production case use the exact intended frozen formula:
 R=sqrt3, v=1/3, Kmix=N-1-j, eta=1/(N^2*Kmix), T=4/3+1/N^2;
 E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)+eta*(N/2-l-1), G_l=T-2E_l;
 rho_l is the positive root of r^2+(H_l-2)*(G_l/T)*r+1-H_l=0, H_l=1+a*c^l;
 r0=rho0*(1-4/N), r1=rho1*(1-4/N), r_l=1 for l>=2;
 sigma0=1, sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i*r_(nu2(i+1));
 Gram_ij=sigma_i*sigma_j*[1/3-eta+sum_(k<j)(2^k/(N-1)-eta)*(-1)^(bit_k(i)+bit_k(j))+eta*N*1_(i=j)].
In this last formula the summation dimension is the CASE depth; use distinct code variable names so the second vertex index is not confused with depth j. Retain all tiny mixed-character terms. The chronological prefix inner-product matrix is A_ij=sum_(k<i)h_k Gram_kj. Then C_ij=A_ij-A_ii+Gram_ij-Gram_ii. Save the full original arrays, not a reduced adjacent or symmetric system. The terminal vertex n is retained; there is no physical h_n.

Use a transparent Bellman-Ford/min-plus difference-constraint analysis with an explicit maximum of N relaxation rounds. At most ONE such feasibility run per production case. A detected cycle must be reconstructed in original index order, and its ACTUAL directed edges, raw weights and raw summed weight must be saved inside that SAME child job. Preserve numerical residuals and all floating defects. For a numerical feasible outcome save the complete potential and max original inequality residual; do not call it exact or a legal lower witness. # Frozen interface and numerical method

Allocation ddd2a22025cdbaac; model/effort supplied as gpt-6-astra / ultra.
This text is written before any numerical import or mathematical evaluation.
All mathematical conclusions remain unverified; this is numerical discovery.

Write P_i=sum_(k<i) h_k v_k and F_i=<z0,v_i>. The normal at z0-P_i
against v_j is <z0-P_i-v_i,v_j-v_i><=0. Expanding gives exactly
F_j-F_i<=<P_i+v_i,v_j-v_i>=C_ij. With Gram_ij=<v_i,v_j> and
A_ij=sum_(k<i)h_k Gram_kj, this is C_ij=A_ij-A_ii+Gram_ij-Gram_ii.
All original ordered pairs are required, including the terminal vertex.
There is no physical step h_n. For the zero vertex the normal is
-F_i+<P_i+v_i,v_i><=0, so a common shift of all F_i pays these finite
lower bounds. The full positive Walsh dictionary and positive radii make
the nonzero vertices independent, so every real vector F is represented by
one actual z0. Thus an original negative cycle excludes every initializer
for this fixed itinerary, not just a constant-blend value law.

Summing F_next-F_current<=C_current,next around a directed cycle gives
0<=sum C_current,next. Conversely, if there is no negative cycle, append
a zero-weight source to every vertex. Every walk can have its nonnegative
cycles deleted; finitely many simple paths attain shortest distances.
Appending any edge to a shortest path proves d_j<=d_i+C_ij. This is a
general exact finite-graph alternative, not a full-depth sign theorem.

The child uses ONE synchronous Bellman-Ford/min-plus run. Starting with
d_i=0, in each round it finds min_i(d_i+C_ij), then retains d_j if no
strict raw floating-point improvement occurs. At most N rounds run.
Each strict improvement records the incoming original edge. No tolerance
is inserted into relaxation, no edge is dropped, and C is never repaired.
If a round has no changes, the full original residual F_j-F_i-C_ij is
reported. After N changing rounds, ONE deterministic predecessor chain
from the smallest changed vertex is followed N times; its directed cycle
is reconstructed, and the original raw weights are summed in the child.
Numerical predecessor anomalies or a nonnegative reconstructed sum remain
unresolved defects rather than triggering a second extraction or retry.

Frozen reporting tolerance: machine epsilon e for binary64; S=max(1,max|C|);
t_edge=256*e*N*S; t_cycle=cycle_edge_count*t_edge. These are reporting
thresholds, not proved roundoff bounds. A cycle with fsum(weights)<-t_cycle
is robustly negative numerically; a raw negative sum within the threshold
is unresolved. A no-cycle-detected candidate requires the maximum ORIGINAL
residual<=t_edge. It is not an exact potential or a legal GD lower witness.
Raw fsum, ordinary sum, numpy sum, individual edge weights and residuals
are retained even when they disagree with a qualitative classification.

The six frozen jobs are the two prescribed fixtures and depths 6,8,9,10.
All four depths use enclosing word parameter M=1536, but dictionary N=2^depth
changes the radial damping and mixed-character weights. They are FINITE
ANALOGUES, not restrictions of N=2^1536 geometry and not depth>=1536.
There is no payoff, radius, blend, stepsize, SDP or benchmark optimization.

The stdlib broker charges all ten seconds and one graph-analysis slot before
launching each child through the existing public mathematical launcher.
Each child runs in a new process group, with all imports, construction,
relaxation, cycle extraction, array saves and numerical diagnostics inside
an externally enforced ten-second deadline. No numerical code runs in the
broker. Calibration failure closes the allocation and retires remaining
credit; every failure counts. Source and case hashes are frozen first.
After close, only saved-text transcription, administrative reads and byte
hashing are allowed. No mathematical extraction or automatic renewal.

# ONE frozen numerical-discovery batch: all-value Gray feasibility via original cycles

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. ONE fresh native invocation, model gpt-6-astra, reasoning ultra, consultOFF. ONE frozen batch, at most SIX charged mathematical child jobs, TEN external hard-timeout seconds each, total SIXTY accounted seconds. At most one complete graph-feasibility analysis per case. No retries, corrections, extra cases or automatic renewal. Two mandatory calibration jobs precede four prescribed discovery jobs; either calibration failure closes the batch and retires unused credit. Formal calls/fact_submit, installations, consults and subagents: ZERO. All older grants are CLOSED and unavailable.

Read OPERATOR.md, full PROBLEM.md, this TASK and exact budget.json. Read the FULL public source research/direct_gray_free_value_level_20261003/RESULT.md (21655 bytes, SHA2562db2648e2e401fd53bbcea1ca3634cd04851609bd81601d9d9957349e667b52c), plus its HANDOFF. The midpoint and two blend bands are excluded there in an UNVERIFIED draft, but arbitrary relative values and vertical initializers are NOT excluded. This is not another lambda scan.

Mathematical interface, to derive briefly as text BEFORE freezing code: given one fixed independent-vertex family v_i and actual cumulative physical displacement P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k v_k, the unknown initial inner products F_i=<z0,v_i> must satisfy
 F_j-F_i <= C_ij, C_ij=<P_i+v_i,v_j-v_i>, for EVERY original i,j.
The nonzero vertices are independent in the full Walsh dictionary, so any real F vector has a unique actual z0. Simultaneous nonzero-vertex normals are equivalent to these difference constraints. Adding the SAME scalar to every F_i preserves them and can pay every zero-vertex lower bound F_i>=<P_i+v_i,v_i>. Thus a negative directed cycle in the ORIGINAL C array rules out EVERY initial point and EVERY relative value choice for this fixed gradient itinerary, independently of constant-blend restrictions. Conversely a numerical potential with small residual is only a numerical candidate, not a proof. A general graph alternative may be proved from path sums, but no full-depth sign conclusion follows from finite data.

The SIX cases are fixed in this order:
1. Feasible directed-constraint fixture C=[[0,2,3],[0,0,-1],[5,3,0]], whose explicit potential F=(0,1,-1) is feasible.
2. The same fixture with C01=-3 instead of2. The original two-edge cycle0->1->0 has total-3. This must be detected with orientation correct. These two fixtures are graph checks, not GD certificates.
3-6. Physical/dictionary depths j=6,8,9,10, always using ONE enclosing M=1536 for the word parameters eps=1/(4M), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps), a=beta/c. Set N=2^j, n=N-1, actual h_i=1+a*c^nu2(i+1), i<n. The dictionary has j bits and N vertices. This is a FINITE ANALOGUE: changing dictionary N changes its radial damping and tiny-character weights; it is NOT the restriction of the full N=2^1536 geometry, NOT physical depth>=1536, and NOT a theorem under the old full-depth hypotheses. Preserve this limitation in every report.

For each production case use the exact intended frozen formula:
 R=sqrt3, v=1/3, Kmix=N-1-j, eta=1/(N^2*Kmix), T=4/3+1/N^2;
 E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)+eta*(N/2-l-1), G_l=T-2E_l;
 rho_l is the positive root of r^2+(H_l-2)*(G_l/T)*r+1-H_l=0, H_l=1+a*c^l;
 r0=rho0*(1-4/N), r1=rho1*(1-4/N), r_l=1 for l>=2;
 sigma0=1, sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i*r_(nu2(i+1));
 Gram_ij=sigma_i*sigma_j*[1/3-eta+sum_(k<j)(2^k/(N-1)-eta)*(-1)^(bit_k(i)+bit_k(j))+eta*N*1_(i=j)].
In this last formula the summation dimension is the CASE depth; use distinct code variable names so the second vertex index is not confused with depth j. Retain all tiny mixed-character terms. The chronological prefix inner-product matrix is A_ij=sum_(k<i)h_k Gram_kj. Then C_ij=A_ij-A_ii+Gram_ij-Gram_ii. Save the full original arrays, not a reduced adjacent or symmetric system. The terminal vertex n is retained; there is no physical h_n.

Use a transparent Bellman-Ford/min-plus difference-constraint analysis with an explicit maximum of N relaxation rounds. At most ONE such feasibility run per production case. A detected cycle must be reconstructed in original index order, and its ACTUAL directed edges, raw weights and raw summed weight must be saved inside that SAME child job. Preserve numerical residuals and all floating defects. For a numerical feasible outcome save the complete potential and max original inequality residual; do not call it exact or a legal lower witness. No payoff optimization, initial-radius optimization, alpha/lambda search, additional parameter choice, SDP or benchmark DP.

Choose and freeze a scale-aware reporting tolerance before the batch; retain raw signed cycle values separately. A robust negative cycle, a tiny unresolved negative quantity, no detected cycle and a timeout are distinct outcomes. Do not repair Gram eigenvalues, average nonsymmetric data, clip signs, alter C, change steps/roots or retry a failing case. No post-close mathematical extraction is allowed. Administrative copying/transcription and byte hashing of saved output is allowed after close.

Execution discipline: write child.py, broker.py, CASES.json and a concise derivation note in THIS directory. The stdlib-only broker must charge each job's full ten accounted seconds BEFORE launching its child. Freeze source/case hashes BEFORE any numerical library import or mathematical evaluation. Each child imports the numerical runtime, constructs every array, performs its one graph analysis, extracts/sums any cycle, computes all diagnostics and writes all mathematical output under a TEN-second external process-group hard timeout. All failures/import failures/timeouts count; no job credit is refunded. Reuse ONLY the existing public mathematical launcher research/run_shared_math_python.sh and existing libraries; do not inspect private workers or install anything. The broker may use stdlib administrative launch/time/hash bookkeeping outside children.

Save TASK/source/case hashes, registration-before-launch records, stdout/stderr, complete original Gram/C/h/sigma/roots data, all solver/algorithm status and cycles/potentials, diagnostics, per-job timeout/exit/wall-time receipts and final closed budget.
